## Watch-to-phone data capture and analysis contract

1. **Preflight:** record test-run/session ID, package/version, OS/API, granted permissions, connection, watch charge/wear state, expected channel set, and fixed timeout. Start through the user-facing app flow; never substitute a shell-started foreground service. Do not scan biomedical channels while charging or claim the watch is worn without confirmation.
2. **Acquire on Wear:** register each available sensor within a bounded stage; preserve the sensor-provided timestamp, original payload/unit declaration, event count, finite sample count, retained preview count, registration outcome, stop reason, and quality/calibration state. Do not replace invalid sensor time with callback receipt time. Stop listeners/triggers and release service resources on success, failure, timeout, and cancellation.
3. **Gate interpretation:** classify no event, condition not met, invalid time/payload, quality rejection, raw-only/unmapped unit, and calibration missing separately. Only compute a physiological result when input identity, units, timebase, quality and device-specific conversion are supported; otherwise retain research-only raw data or withhold the result.
4. **Transport and persist:** attach the same session/run ID and per-channel sample timestamp to the watch snapshot; track send, retry, ordering/payload rejection, phone receipt, persistence outcome, and pending/superseded state separately. On phone, retain watch measurement time separately from phone receipt/publication time; then verify stored state after app/process restart. Unit/source/coverage mismatches must not be merged into a single score.
5. **Freshness and UI:** derive the displayed state from measurement time, never export/packet receipt time. The app has a 30-minute generic raw-sensor display window and 10-minute raw Wear research-input window, both software policies rather than clinical standards. Health Connect metric freshness remains unconfigured; retain SpO₂ records as dated references without current-value classification or scoring until the window is approved and tested.
6. **Analyze and report:** report counts and failure reasons before any formula output. The run denominator is all predeclared initiated tests, including cancellations and failures; report complete success, partial completion, failure, cancellation and unknown separately. A sensor event can count as acquired while its physiological interpretation remains unavailable. Do not report “100% success” from a small or selected sample.

**Review flags requiring evidence:** static audit plus targeted tests confirm sleep sessions are grouped by local wake date, overlapping sessions invalidate the aggregate, and incomplete stage coverage prevents sleep-score use; suspected app-side cross-midnight misattribution is not reproduced. AndroidX documents `TimeRangeFilter.between(Instant, Instant)` as `[start,end)` and the sleep guide models `SleepSessionRecord` as a session interval with stages, but the cited docs do not state how a provider returns a session that only partially intersects the query interval. Therefore live provider boundary behavior remains an integration check, not a resolved assumption ([TimeRangeFilter](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/time/TimeRangeFilter), [sleep sessions](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-connect/features/sleep-sessions)). Freshness windows are mapped to separate display/conclusion/source roles but still lack validation rationale. Current devices report USB charging, so no fresh paired-device run was performed.

## 2026-09-28 lifecycle defect and verification

- Evidence: `RawVitalsProbe.start()` handled `SecurityException` only around trigger/listener registration and sensor flush; another runtime registration/flush exception could escape instead of becoming a per-channel terminal result.
- Fix: `SensorRegistrationGuard` now maps permission failures to `permission_denied` and other `RuntimeException`s to `registration_exception`; both use the probe's existing cleanup/diagnostic terminal path. Listener and trigger cleanup proceeds independently if one cleanup action throws. The direct sensor and PPG measurement entry points now send a terminal failure snapshot and report its reason, matching the full-scan behavior. No timestamp, sensor value, unit, calibration, or physiology logic changed.
- Tests/builds: four-case guard suite passes; full mobile 392 and Wear 137 unit tests pass (zero failures/errors); both Release assemblies and Release lint tasks pass. A combined filtered/full Gradle invocation initially yielded only the four filtered Wear tests; the unfiltered Wear suite was rerun separately and passed 137/137.
- Limitation: tests prove exception-to-terminal-state routing and cleanup iteration, and compile the single-measurement result path; they do not reproduce a vendor sensor-manager exception or validate listener/WakeLock lifecycle on hardware. This patch was not installed after these changes and no scan was run; previously installed hashes refer to prior builds. Wear Release remains unsigned: SHA-256 `c67a381f41cf3479df27c5b186e18c9322afff0c65bc864dd2b4b8978a0078aa`.

## 2026-09-28 wall/monotonic timestamp mapping guard

- Evidence: `RawVitalsProbe` maps monotonic `SensorEvent.timestamp` into epoch time using a wall-clock/elapsed-realtime offset captured at probe start. Android documents wall time as user/network adjustable and potentially discontinuous, while elapsed realtime is monotonic ([SystemClock API](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/os/SystemClock)). Reusing the old offset after a wall-clock step could give an otherwise real raw sample a false epoch timestamp.
- Fix: `SensorEventEpochTimeResolver` compares the current wall/elapsed offset to the captured one. If drift exceeds 1,000 ms, it keeps the raw sample but emits epoch time `0` with source `epoch_offset_drift_exceeded`; the phone detail screen explains that the timestamp is withheld. The 1 s tolerance is a software timing/jitter guard, not a clinical threshold.
- Verification: forward/backward clock-step and tolerance-boundary tests pass; mobile `SensorDisplayStateTest` and `SensorSnapshotTimestampTest` pass. Full mobile 392 and Wear 139 unit tests pass (zero failures/errors); both Release assemblies and Release lint tasks pass. The generated Release APKs are unsigned (`apksigner verify` reports missing signing manifest); SHA-256: mobile `A31377231969D95758679250C61C7FC9EB2CBB2D930670102D25FCD837A8A4BB`, Wear `1E2FC3CD5731EF4A228FFD3B34CF2DFE281E14B30FAF1330A1EF6C683D82808A`.
- Limitation: no device install or scan; this confirms the timestamp pipeline fails closed in unit-tested resolver/UI code, not that a real OS time adjustment was observed or the reason survives full Wear→phone transport. Paired-device verification remains open.

## Shared decisions / non-goals

- No new algorithm or UI feature is added until packages 1–4 establish trustworthy inputs and failure states.
- No diagnosis, medical-grade claim, or physiological conversion is enabled from raw PPG, ECG, SpO₂, or BioZ absent device-specific mapping, timebase, calibration, and validation.
- A build/test pass, recent export timestamp, permission query, or AI consensus is not a successful sensor measurement.
- External-AI review, if separately requested, uses only a minimal sanitized technical summary and does not receive raw signals, device IDs, credentials, or repository files.
- Keep each delivery batch limited to one primary acceptance objective; cross-layer edits are grouped only when needed to preserve a consistent contract.
- Banister coefficient source: [J Appl Physiol (2003), DOI 10.1152/japplphysiol.00482.2003](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00482.2003). This supports the formula coefficients only; it does not validate this watch's HR inputs or predict clinical outcomes.

## Research decision: model transferability limits

- Related Google and consumer-wearable studies use trained, cohort-specific models and defined protocols; they are research context, not a portable calibration formula. The Nature insulin-resistance study trained against HOMA-IR using wearable time series plus demographics and routine blood biomarkers, with an independent validation cohort; it does not establish that a Xiaomi Watch 2 Pro-only heuristic can estimate or diagnose insulin resistance ([Nature, DOI 10.1038/s41586-026-10179-2](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-026-10179-2)).
- WavesFM describes a hierarchical pretrained representation for longitudinal wearable waveforms, not a device calibration or standalone clinical decision rule ([arXiv:2605.09173](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09173)).
- The cited blood-pressure work uses a specific PPG+accelerometer model and Pixel Watch 3 study protocol compared with ambulatory BP; it remains a **medRxiv preprint**, not evidence of validated Xiaomi Watch 2 Pro blood-pressure measurement ([medRxiv, DOI 10.64898/2025.12.10.25341972](https://doi.org/10.64898/2025.12.10.25341972)).
- These sources do not verify the proprietary implementation or inputs of the Google Health Guardian product. Do not claim their methods are Health Guardian's exact model, transplant them to this watch, or present outputs as diagnosis without device-specific evidence and validation.

## First execution slice

- Research-data objective delivered: created a versioned dataset registry and offline benchmark harness for PhysioNet Wrist PPG During Exercise. Benchmark v3 adds participant-level metrics across 8 participants / 218 windows: macro-MAE 38.52 bpm (peak count) and 44.93 bpm (spectral); pooled MAE 38.18 and 41.94 bpm. Both stay negative controls. A literature-inspired transfer screen rejected 218/218 windows; without expert SQI labels this is not a validated quality assessment. Local data is ignored and not packaged; twelve benchmark tests currently pass.
- Shared engine foundation: extracted duplicated mobile/Wear HRV, optical research, pressure-altitude, and guarded stress formulas into pure Kotlin/JVM `:health-engine`. Both apps now call the same implementation; acquisition/UI/platform adapters remain separate. Added core-level regression tests. Targeted phone/Wear tests and Debug builds passed. This does not unify all algorithm families or establish physiological/clinical validity.
- Corrected risk discovered during migration: shared research stress scoring rejects non-finite/out-of-domain nRMSSD and HR values instead of clamping invalid inputs to a plausible score; regression tests exist in both app modules and the core module.
- Benchmark integrity review found silent missing-record skips and unchecked manifest paths. Both are fixed: out-of-root checksum/record paths are rejected, and missing record files are explicit failures. Rerun processed 19/19 records; 59/59 checksums verified; eleven harness tests pass. The candidate interval-regularity rule can reject true irregular physiology, so it is not a rhythm/quality diagnosis and must not suppress independent rhythm review.
- Remaining next steps: obtain expert SQI labels or a dataset with validated signal-quality annotations before calibrating/rejecting on a learned SQI; predeclare participant-level evaluation protocol before tuning; expand dataset-license registry; define consent/ethics/reference-device pairing before personal-data collection. Mobile/Wear full tests and Debug/Release builds pass for current Android sources; benchmark edits do not touch Android code. No device was connected and no APK was installed or uploaded.

Package 0 now has an explicitly dated, scoped source snapshot fingerprint and matching current APK hashes/build results; Git revision remains unavailable and release signing identity is missing. Do not claim Git history or installability. Continue package 1 source-ID/provenance and score-consumer evidence while seeking authorized signing configuration and paired-device availability. No real-device acceptance is claimed.

- WP3 cancellation/cleanup hardening: sensor-suite delayed callbacks now share a removable Handler tag and are cancelled on session cleanup; stale suite tokens are invalidated on normal finish. Probe shutdown isolates failures so every probe is attempted and ECG measurement leases are released in `finally`. Wear unit tests and debug assembly pass; paired-device runtime behavior remains unverified.
- WP1 score-consumer audit: recovery, readiness, and body-energy heuristics now reject missing or out-of-range (outside 0–100) inputs instead of emitting clamped scores. Independent review also caught the reported body-battery path bypassing that guard; it now rejects out-of-range reported scores, with regression coverage. A later audit found same-origin overlapping HeartRateRecord intervals were still flattened and sample-weighted; HR records now pass the shared non-overlap gate before contributing to statistics or downstream calculations. Existing overlap-policy tests cover overlapping and exact repeated intervals. This does not validate the heuristic clinically; UI continues to identify calculations as local estimates. Both test suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; real-device/UI acceptance remains open.
- WP1 energy estimate fix: prior display combined a full-day Mifflin basal estimate with active calories accrued only so far, overstating morning totals while calling them a daily estimate. Basal contribution is now prorated by elapsed fraction of the local calendar day (accounting for 23/25-hour DST days), and the UI says “截至目前”. Invalid profiles, unknown sex coefficient, and invalid active-calorie inputs remain unavailable. Targeted tests plus both app test suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; independent validation of the estimate is not claimed.
- WP2 score-display audit: unsupported detail metrics previously fell through to an invented fixed 50/100 “public clinical comparison” and marked it trusted. Fallback now explicitly states no applicable score/reference model, marks it untrusted, and the UI hides numeric scores for untrusted results. Both app test suites and Debug/Release builds pass; remaining metric-by-metric calibration and UI acceptance remain open.
- WP1 source-ID audit: Health Connect record IDs survived typed reads but were discarded when pages were combined and projected into chart points. Both the shared paginated reader and metric-detail reader now reject blank or duplicate IDs (without value/time fuzzy deduplication); added identity-policy coverage. This avoids duplicate point weighting as well as cumulative double-counting. Targeted policy, full mobile/Wear unit suites, and Debug/Release builds pass; live-provider duplicate behavior is not device-verified.
- WP2 point-data integrity: metric detail previously silently dropped point records with out-of-window timestamps, and did not reject non-finite values/blank origins before chart statistics. It now shows a distinct integrity-failure state instead of conflating invalid data with no records/provider failure. Added tests for finite values, required source, inclusive start/exclusive end, and interval distinction. Targeted test, both unit suites, and Debug/Release builds pass; provider fault injection and device UI remain pending.
- WP2 Health Center read-state integrity: summary previously collapsed denied permission, permission-query failure, provider error, and successful empty result into the same “authorization/Health Connect not ready” message. It now checks each record permission first and displays separate empty, denied, permission-query-failed, read-failed, and successful-count states; cancellation is preserved. Added policy tests. Targeted test, both unit suites, and Debug/Release builds pass; permission-settings round-trip still needs device/UI verification.
- WP2 pagination integrity: the generic Health Connect reader now rejects any repeated page token immediately, instead of continuing until the 25,000-record cap. The detail-page collector shares the same cycle detector; added token-cycle/terminal-token tests. Both unit suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; live provider paging behavior remains unverified.
- WP2 permission-state integrity: Health Connect permission-query exceptions no longer escape as a generic analysis crash or look like denied permission/empty data. The reader skips data queries when permission state is unknown and reports a distinct query-failure reason; added policy tests for granted, denied, and query-failed states. Both unit suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; provider failure behavior remains device-unverified.
- WP4 timestamp provenance: the watch packet previously exposed only its send/computation time, so a 7-day-window input could look newly measured. The phone now carries RHR, HRV, completed sleep-session time, latest wrist-temperature sample time, and the exact TRIMP-contributing exercise interval bounds separately from calculation completion time; future/ongoing sleep end times are excluded. Wear rejects older/equal insight packets, and UI labels missing/available core-input times plus the 7-day activity-load window. Residual: source-time propagation for some phone-only metric inputs (including SpO₂ and daily aggregate records) remains incomplete; all metric freshness thresholds stay `UNCONFIGURED`, so no current/clinical freshness claim is made. Both unit suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; Data Layer runtime behavior still needs paired-device verification.
- WP2 inferred-sleep freshness: review found the HR-based fallback scanned samples from the full seven-day query; its `now` argument was unused, allowing an older sleep-like segment to populate recent sleep. It now evaluates only samples in the trailing 30 hours, returns the contributing sample interval, and labels the result as HR-inferred rather than a recorded sleep session. Regression tests accept a recent 3-hour segment and reject a longer segment outside the window. This remains a research heuristic, not a sleep-stage or clinical determination. Both unit suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; device timing remains unverified.
- WP4 SpO₂ timestamp consistency: evidence review found invalid percentages were excluded from summary statistics while their timestamps could still define the displayed observation interval. The interval now comes only from valid Health Connect 0–100% samples; any returned record outside the requested seven-day cutoff-to-now query invalidates the entire interval/summary and is reported as an integrity issue. Added regressions for invalid values, valid-sample time bounds, and all-invalid input. Focused test and full mobile (384) / Wear (133) unit suites pass; mobile Release assembly and lint pass. This does not configure freshness thresholds or establish device accuracy.
- WP4 future timestamp classification: evidence found the UI classifier called future measurement times “stale” while export separately reported `future_timestamp`, and freshness summaries did not count future timestamps as attention-worthy. Added a dedicated future-timestamp state used by detail/UI, Health Center, metric-summary JSON, and sensor-suite export; signed `age_ms` remains negative and future-timestamp records are excluded from fresh-raw counts. Added explicit total/current-session export counters plus regressions for classifier/export parity and attention behavior. Full mobile (385) / Wear (133) unit suites pass; mobile Release assembly/lint pass. Hardware remains on charge; no scan was run.
- WP4 Health Connect source-time bounds: RHR, HRV, wrist-temperature and SpO₂ point records now receive an explicit post-query timestamp-range check; any record before the requested seven-day start or after the query cutoff withholds that metric's full record set and surfaces an integrity reason instead of allowing it into calculations. Added inclusive-boundary and just-outside-window tests. Full mobile (386) / Wear (133) unit suites, both Release assemblies and both Release lint tasks pass. This checks query-window integrity only; per-metric freshness thresholds and device acceptance remain open.
- WP5 raw-export consent preview: the Health Center previously showed a generic consent warning before opening Android's share sheet. It now calculates and previews the actual saved sensor-channel count, sample/latest/waveform/source-waveform point totals, oldest/newest available measurement times, plaintext recipient handoff, and best-effort 15-minute cache retention before the user confirms. Added summary-policy tests for measured/empty channels, point counts, and missing timestamps. Full mobile (388) / Wear (133) unit suites, both Release builds and both Release lint tasks pass. It does not encrypt the export; Keystore encryption and key lifecycle remain release blockers.
- WP5 partial plaintext export handling: the engineering ADB export wrote directly to its final path, so an I/O failure could leave a truncated JSON file. Both ADB export and Health Center share-export now write/fsync a unique same-directory temp file and atomically replace the final path; exceptions remove the temp, and tests prove an earlier complete export survives a failed rewrite. Engineering diagnostics also delete crash-leftover temp files on the next debug-screen open; Health Center cleans its temps on the next export. Focused retention tests and full mobile (391) / Wear (133) suites pass; both Release builds and both lint tasks pass. This mitigates partial-file residue but does not encrypt data or guarantee time-based cleanup after process death.

## Incremental audit — temperature semantics (2026-09-28)

- Evidence: `WearControlService` forwards vendor-reported temperature with an optional manual offset but no verifiable watch-level unit/calibration provenance; downstream previously displayed `°C`, compared against wrist-skin reference bands, and included the score in the sensor-suite composite.
- Change: temperature conclusion/detail now labels readings as research/device-reported, omits unit and clinical reference comparisons, assigns no health score, and excludes research-only temperature from composite quality scoring. Raw measurement remains visible as an unverified device value.
- Verification: `:mobile:testDebugUnitTest :wear:testDebugUnitTest :mobile:assembleDebug` passed. This is code/test evidence only; no ADB executable/device is available in this environment, so UI/hardware behavior is not accepted.
- Follow-up evidence: detail aggregation previously summed same-origin overlapping sleep sessions, which could double-count a night. Period summaries now reject overlapping sessions and present a specific explanation instead of a cumulative chart/daily mean. Added overlap regression test; the same mobile/Wear tests and mobile Debug build passed again.
- Independent review: a reviewer hypothesized that daily sleep bypasses the overlap guard; source check disproved this because `cumulative` is true for every sleep period, including day view. The query filters and chart both bucket sessions by wake/end time, matching the documented wake-date rule. Reviewer did identify that scan-channel coverage still contributed to the numeric suite score even after research-only temperature was excluded from the quality average.
- Follow-up fix: suite score now reflects only included modality signal-quality scores; raw sensor coverage is displayed separately and cannot raise that score. Research-only readings produce a research result without a numeric score, including per-modality rows. Added tests for coverage invariance and research-only inputs. Full mobile/Wear unit suites and mobile Debug assembly pass again.
- Independent review of the Health Connect temperature insight found that it included all measurement locations and compared a 7-day window against a 28-day wrist-temperature reference. The official Health Connect record exposes `measurementLocation` including wrist; source: [Android API reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/records/BodyTemperatureMeasurementLocation).
- Follow-up fix: the descriptive temperature trend now accepts wrist-location records only, aggregates to daily medians, requires four distinct recorded days, and compares the latest day with prior observed days; removed the mismatched 28-day clinical-reference comparison. Test covers minimum days and within-day outlier resistance. Full mobile/Wear unit suites and mobile Debug assembly pass after the change.
- Release preflight: `:mobile:assembleRelease :wear:assembleRelease` also passes. Both release build types have `signingConfig = null` because release credentials are absent; builds are not installable release candidates. No device acceptance or publication.
- WP0 source baseline: recorded a SHA-256 digest for 320 scoped source/config files and the matching v145/3.13.35 mobile/Wear release APK hashes in `docs/build-provenance-2026-09-28.md`. SDK-bundled `adb devices -l` is empty; both release APKs report `DOES NOT VERIFY`. This improves byte-level traceability but does not replace Git revision, signer identity, runtime proof, or a signed release.
- WP1 Mifflin correction: `DailyEnergyEstimate` previously used a homemade `-78` sex offset for `UNSPECIFIED`; it now returns unavailable rather than emit an unsupported estimate. Added a regression test. Mobile/Wear unit tests, mobile Debug assembly, and both Release assemblies passed; refreshed source/APK hashes are in the provenance file. (Source snapshot contains 319 files.)
- WP1 independent-review reconciliation: a reviewer alleged daily step regularity could mix origins. Source verification disproved the issue: the shared `SourceIntervalAggregation.assess` called by `onlyUnambiguousIntervals` rejects blank/multiple origins before daily grouping. No code change warranted; existing cross-origin policy tests cover the rejecting helper, though no end-to-end Health Connect query test exists.
- WP1 score semantics: renamed the fixed-8-hour sleep-duration heuristic and sleep+stress readiness composite across phone/watch UI; removed readiness-like training-status language. Added bounds/rounding tests for the sleep heuristic. Phone and Wear unit tests plus Debug and Release assemblies pass. No connected device; release APKs remain unsigned and are not installable release candidates.
- WP1 dashboard aggregate parity: Home had shown Health Connect daily step, distance, and active-calorie aggregates without validating origin/overlap, unlike the stricter algorithm insights path. It now accepts each only when the aggregate has one origin and exactly matches eligible, non-overlapping same-origin raw intervals; otherwise it hides the number and states why. Added policy tests. Both unit suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass; no device-connected acceptance.
- WP1 remaining Home totals: elevation and floors were directly summed across records, and hydration across all origins. Elevation/floors now use the existing single-origin, valid/non-overlapping interval gate; hydration requires a nonblank single origin and positive finite values. Rejected aggregates/trends are hidden with an explanation. Both unit suites, Debug builds and Release builds pass. Hydration duplicate-entry semantics and the other Health Connect metric paths remain under review; no real-device acceptance.
- WP1 heart-rate summary: Home min/max previously merged samples from every Health Connect origin. It now hides the range unless intervals are valid, one-source, non-overlapping, and contain usable samples. Official Health Connect docs classify hydration as an interval representing a drink, consistent with single-origin aggregation; duplicate identity still depends on upstream record IDs. Both mobile/Wear unit suites and Debug/Release assemblies pass. This does not establish live-data correctness.
- WP1 per-metric origin fix: `AggregationResult.dataOrigins` describes the contributing origins for the result, not an independently queryable source list per metric. Home now validates each aggregate against that metric's own raw-record origins and total, avoiding false rejection when unrelated metrics have different sources. Sleep duration is similarly gated against same-source, non-overlapping sessions assigned by wake date. Full mobile/Wear unit tests and Debug/Release assemblies pass; ADB remains empty, so no live verification.
- WP1 trend consistency: oxygen saturation and weight trend sparklines previously mixed all sources while the headline value selected one latest record. They now plot only the latest record's origin; weight identifies that source. Added source-filter policy regression test; full mobile/Wear tests and Debug/Release builds pass. Health Connect API `AggregationResult.dataOrigins` is documented as contributing origins for the result; per-metric validation continues to rely on that metric's raw records.
- WP2 bounded pagination: independent review confirmed sleep detail intentionally assigns the full session to its wake date (documented in the UI and covered by cross-midnight tests), so the suspected range-edge behavior is not a defect. It did identify repeated next-page tokens only failed at the overall page limit; collection now detects a token cycle immediately and rejects the entire result. Added regression test asserting only two fetches. Full mobile/Wear test suites and Debug/Release builds pass. Device-level Health Connect behavior remains unverified.
- WP2 cumulative coverage: extracted the interval/cumulative metric policy and tested that step, distance, active-calorie, and sleep details all pass through the shared origin/overlap guard (point metrics remain non-cumulative). This closes the prior test-coverage gap for distance/calorie wiring; it is policy coverage, not instrumentation of Health Connect itself. Full mobile/Wear unit suites and Debug/Release builds pass.
- WP1 source-choice review: removed a pre-existing Xiaomi-name preference when a detail page chooses its default origin. It now retains a still-valid user choice, otherwise picks the lexically first origin consistently; tests confirm no brand preference. This follows the plan's no-unverified-brand-priority rule. Full mobile/Wear test suites and Debug/Release builds pass; UI/device acceptance remains open.
- WP2 permission recovery: detail-page permission-denied state now links to Health Connect settings and automatically retries on return; unresolved settings activity shows a short message. No-data and provider-error states remain distinct. Both mobile/Wear unit suites and Debug/Release builds pass; settings round-trip still needs device/UI verification.
- WP2 sleep duration integrity: awake-stage intervals are now clipped to the containing sleep session and unioned before subtraction, preventing overlapping Awake/Out-of-bed stages from being counted twice. Added overlap-plus-boundary regression test. Full mobile/Wear test suites and Debug/Release builds pass; actual OEM stage behavior remains unverified.
- Sleep inference review follow-up: Wear sleep glance now distinguishes Health Connect sleep records from low-HR spans (“非睡眠紀錄”); ambiguous/recent sessions and failed or unauthorized sleep queries prevent HR fallback. Policy tests cover each state. Targeted sleep-policy test, both mobile/Wear test suites, and Debug/Release assemblies pass. ADB lists no devices; on-device UI/sync behavior remains unverified.
- Sleep inference integrity: low-heart-rate spans remain research observations, not sleep records, and are excluded from sleep scoring/readiness/recovery/body-energy and sleep-deficit advice. Recent/non-future sample bounds are tested. The follow-up below addresses prior reviewer findings on Wear labeling and fallback eligibility; paired-device UI/sync evidence is still pending.

# Progress note — research dataset governance

- Expanded the dataset registry to five entries and added automated checks for required rights metadata and the no-APK-bundling policy.
- Reviewed primary publisher/license pages for ODC-By, CC BY 4.0, and restricted MIMIC-IV access. Commercial reuse of a database license is not treated as clearance for every underlying item or a medical-product claim.
- No additional datasets or user health data were uploaded, downloaded, or sent to external AI. Next: verify each selected dataset's archive-level notices and benchmark protocol before fetching more data; then define a consented, reference-instrument paired Xiaomi collection protocol with privacy/ethics and regulatory review gates.

## Paired-device validation planning — 2026-09-28

## WP1 health-insight input integrity — 2026-09-28

- Independent source audit found three concrete score-input issues: stress could combine different-date RHR/HRV; its activity-load bucket treated unobserved days like zero; the sleep score used session duration without removing recorded awake stages.
- Fix: stress now requires RHR/HRV from the same source and local date, selects the newest date with a valid pair and then the closest timestamp pair within that date. Seven-day active-calorie load is eligible only when source intervals continuously cover every entire local day (including DST-length days); interval records alone do not prove complete coverage. Half-open intervals ending exactly at midnight remain attributable to the prior day. Sleep-duration scores require continuous known stage coverage for every session; otherwise only the raw session window is shown with an explicit caveat, not used as actual sleep duration or scored. Recorded awake/out-of-bed intervals are unioned and subtracted when stages fully cover the session.
- UI measurement-time text now describes the pairing rule and full-day coverage requirement; stress input times are blank when no eligible pair exists. Added regression tests for source/date pairing, complete interval coverage, midnight/DST boundaries, awake-stage subtraction, incomplete/unknown sleep stages, and window clipping.
- Verification: targeted regression tests, full `:mobile:testDebugUnitTest`, and `:mobile:assembleDebug` passed. No ADB/device is available, so live Health Connect/provider behavior and UI acceptance are not verified. Same-day pairing and fixed score rules are engineering integrity gates, not clinically validated thresholds; all results remain non-diagnostic.
- Research registry follow-up: tests now validate HTTPS landing/license/download/manifest URLs, ISO review dates, required scope/rights fields, populated limitations, and that model transfer is not planned absent a rights/privacy review. An independent internal reviewer caught an imprecise assertion that overstated this as a categorical prohibition; corrected the assertion and its name. The complete Python research suite passes 16 tests. The rerun remains a failing transfer baseline: peak-count pooled MAE 38.18 bpm, spectral pooled MAE 41.94 bpm, and the exploratory gate rejects all 218 windows; this cannot calibrate Xiaomi or demonstrate clinical accuracy.
- Follow-up independent code review caught an insufficient seven-day completeness condition, a midnight half-open interval edge case, and unclear sleep-stage semantics. Tightened active-calorie eligibility to exact continuous coverage of each full local-day interval, corrected midnight classification, and prevented incomplete/unknown stage data from entering the sleep-duration score while retaining a caveated session-window display. Added regression tests including DST-short/long local days. A second independent review confirmed the fixes and phone-to-watch field propagation. Full mobile/Wear unit suites, both Release builds, and both Release lint tasks pass. Official Health Connect docs state stage intervals need not be continuous, so their mere presence is not evidence of full sleep-stage coverage.
- Release/device recheck: `apksigner verify` still reports both Release APKs unsigned (`Missing META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`); standard Android SDK ADB exists but `adb devices -l` returns no devices. No install, UI/device check, live Health Connect scan, or paired-device verification was performed. Package 6 and release remain blocked on a valid signing identity and connected paired devices.

- Added `docs/PAIRED_WATCH_VALIDATION_PROTOCOL_DRAFT_2026-09-28.md` with channel-specific feasibility gates, consent/privacy/ethics gates, synchronized acquisition, reference-device traceability, locked analysis, coverage accounting, and acceptance-plan requirements.
- This is a protocol draft only: no enrollment or data collection. It intentionally leaves performance thresholds and sample size unset pending intended-use, qualified statistician/clinical, reference-equipment, ethics, and regulatory review.
- Current implication: only outputs with verified watch-side units/timestamps may enter physiological paired comparison. Uncalibrated raw SpO₂/ECG/BioZ/PPG cannot be promoted by this protocol; first prove channel and sampling semantics. No app or firmware changes were made.
- Independent agent review was attempted but unavailable because the agent concurrency limit was reached; no consensus/review claim is made.

## Research harness verification — 2026-09-28

- Independent read-only agent audited source-origin and overlap handling in `SourceIntervalAggregation` and `AlgorithmicInsightsEngine`; no reproducible defect was found. This review did not access health exports or device data.

The PhysioNet manifest verified all 59 expected files (no missing files, checksum mismatches, or invalid paths); 19/19 listed records were processed and 218 reference-valid windows evaluated.

These errors show the current baselines are not suitable as pulse-rate outputs. The exploratory quality gate accepted 0/218 windows for both methods, so gated MAE is undefined; do not relax its threshold to manufacture coverage. Per-participant summaries are reported, but this dataset is only 8 participants and does not validate Xiaomi or clinical performance.
- Independent benchmark review flagged that ECG annotation indices and PPG timestamps use relative zero without applied offset correction. The dataset paper reports up to 1.21 s recording-duration mismatch and suggests ignoring it for coarse averaged-rate comparisons; it does not justify beat-level alignment. The JSON report now carries a machine-readable `alignment_assessment` (`relative_zero_assumed_unverified`) and explicitly says no correction was applied; an assertion test prevents that limitation from disappearing. This metadata change does not alter estimates or scores.
- Reverification: all 14 research registry/benchmark unit tests pass, and the complete benchmark was rerun with the same checksums and baseline results. Result artifact remains `.local-research-data/results/wrist-ppg-benchmark.json`.
