## ECG conclusion sample sufficiency — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `HealthConclusionEngine.ecgConclusion` previously let any positive raw sample count reach `GOOD` when quality/RR/HR gates passed, while HRV's ECG path already required at least 32 samples. An independent agent identified this inconsistency; source inspection confirmed it.
- Fix: shared ECG conclusion classifier now requires at least 32 raw samples before any graded conclusion. Smaller recent captures remain visible as research data, receive no score, and cannot contribute a positive summary.
- Acceptance: regression test uses 8 fresh samples with otherwise favorable quality/RR/HR inputs and asserts research-only, zero score. Focused `HealthConclusionEngineTest` passed; complete `:mobile:testDebugUnitTest :wear:testDebugUnitTest` passed.
- Verification: after the fix, both mobile and Wear unit suites passed; mobile Debug and Release assemblies passed. The rebuilt Release APK remains unsigned (`apksigner`: `DOES NOT VERIFY`) and was not installed. No ADB/device acceptance was performed.
- Remaining: 32 samples is an existing engineering completeness floor, not a clinical-validity threshold. Audit also found temperature surfaces still expose a `°C`-named field/label and the watch still applies Celsius offsets despite the documented unit-unverified policy; correct that cross-layer inconsistency next.

## Temperature unit-integrity correction — 2026-09-28

- Source audit confirmed raw Xiaomi/Wear temperature HAL values were treated as Celsius in the mobile self-test, a calibration profile UI, Health Connect writes, and older app-origin Health Connect records. No device-level unit mapping or end-to-end calibration evidence supported those conversions.
- Removed the app's raw-temperature-to-Health-Connect body-temperature write path and removed raw temperature from supported Health Connect metrics. Watch capture now retains finite raw values, explicitly transports `temperature_unit_status=unverified`, and does not apply offsets. Phone/watch UI, feature catalog, navigation, export, and self-test label the value raw and unscored; temperature calibration profiles are no longer supported. Home, metric detail, and algorithmic insights exclude legacy records written by this app so they cannot reappear as trusted Celsius data.
- Added regression coverage for raw finite values outside Celsius ranges, invalid/no-event distinctions, self-test claims, and raw-only conclusions. Full mobile and Wear unit suites passed; mobile/Wear Debug and Release assemblies passed. Release APK hashes are updated in `docs/build-provenance-2026-09-28.md`.
- Not verified: no ADB executable/device session was available in this turn, so no fresh sensor scan, UI, sync, battery, or paired-reference test was performed. Raw values remain research-only; this change does not validate sensor identity, unit, calibration, or clinical performance.
- Historical status correction: an earlier check reported the benchmark blocked in the bundled Python runtime. The isolated `.local-research-data/venv` is available; current registry+benchmark tests pass (16), and a fresh run succeeds with 59/59 checksums, 19/19 records, and 218 windows. No new dataset was downloaded or bundled in this run.

## Wear RR screening sufficiency — 2026-09-28

- A delegated reviewer flagged possible 3–4 RR intervals reaching screening with missing HRV features replaced by zero. Source verification disproved that exact path: shared `HealthAlgorithmEngine.hrvFromRr` returns null statistics below five valid intervals, and `WearRhythmScreening` already returned insufficient when mean RR was null. However, its advertised “at least 3 RR” message contradicted the actual five-interval floor, and `?: 0.0` fallbacks could silently make future partial feature results appear regular.
- Fix: require five valid RR intervals explicitly, report current valid count, and return insufficient if any required statistic is absent instead of replacing it with zero. Out-of-range intervals do not count toward the minimum.
- Regression tests cover 3/4 valid intervals and four valid plus one out-of-range interval. Targeted Wear rhythm tests, both full mobile/Wear unit suites, and Wear Debug/Release assemblies passed.
- This verifies a software sufficiency gate only. It does not validate the rhythm heuristic, AF sensitivity/specificity, ECG measurement, or clinical use; live-device acceptance remains open.

## Watch sample-time provenance — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `SensorSnapshotTimestamp.sampleTime` fell back to the phone-visible Data Layer publication timestamp when a legacy watch snapshot lacked `last_sample_at_ms`; `WearDataProcessor` also labeled this `legacy_publication_time_fallback`. This could make an old measurement appear recent. Publication time remains separately stored for transport ordering/diagnostics and is not measurement time.
- Fix: absent watch sample time now stays `0`/unknown, with timestamp source `watch_sample_timestamp_missing`. Raw payloads remain inspectable, but the shared display/export classifier marks them `TIMESTAMP_UNKNOWN`; they do not count as fresh. No Health Connect freshness window was inferred.
- Regression test proves a recent publication timestamp cannot turn a legacy timestamp-less payload into fresh data. Targeted timestamp/display-state tests and both full mobile/Wear suites plus Debug/Release builds passed.
- WP4 remains incomplete: per-metric Health Connect freshness windows are still unconfigured; paired device/session acceptance is required before configuring them.

## Paired validation metadata gate — 2026-09-28

- Added a metadata-only paired-session JSON Schema and standard-library preflight validator. It checks approval/consent records, pseudonymous identifiers, app build hashes, documented measurement channel/unit/source, timezone-aware chronology, calibrated reference coverage, predeclared synchronization tolerance, worn/not-charging condition, and encrypted/checksummed artifact metadata.
- Raw-only observations can be recorded as non-comparison eligible; no conversion, enrollment, capture, or upload is enabled. Direct identity fields and unknown extra fields are rejected. A passing record means only metadata preflight passed, not ethics approval, clinical validity, or permission to collect.
- Added synthetic metadata-only regression tests for approval/consent, identity rejection, calibration/mapping, synchronization, encryption, and raw-only non-comparison behavior. Verification: complete clinical-data research suite passes (26 tests); both Python files pass `py_compile`. No paired physiological data was collected or uploaded.

## Health Connect identity failure clarity — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `readAllRecords` rejects blank/duplicate Health Connect record IDs and metric-detail reads apply the same gate, but `AlgorithmicInsightsEngine` caught every provider/query exception as generic “read failed”; its user-visible reason hid the integrity rejection category.
- Fix: added a small failure-description policy that reports duplicate/blank record identity as “拒絕合併”, while keeping unrelated provider errors generic and not exposing exception details.
- Verification: focused mobile regression test plus full `:mobile:testDebugUnitTest :wear:testDebugUnitTest` passed. No health records were read for this source-level test and no device/provider runtime behavior was verified.
- WP1 remains open: audit every composite score/metric consumer and source/overlap behavior; this change does not establish missing record IDs or data-source correctness in a live provider.
- Release check after source edits: `:mobile:assembleRelease :wear:assembleRelease :mobile:lintRelease :wear:lintRelease` passed. Both generated artifacts are explicitly `*-release-unsigned.apk`; `apksigner verify` fails with missing `META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`. No install/publish was performed; release remains blocked on authorized signing and device acceptance.

## Body Energy dependency review — 2026-09-28

- Correction to the prior audit hypothesis: `ans.bodyBattery` is a locally computed ANS heuristic, not a device-reported battery measurement. It is returned directly when available and does not mix in sleep; its RHR/HRV/activity inputs must share one source. The sleep/stress fallback requires consistent provenance. Neither score is a validated physiological readiness measurement.
- Rejected the extra sleep-origin gate before release; Body Energy remains governed by the existing ANS/activity source gate. Recovery and Readiness continue to require sleep/ANS provenance alignment because those formulas combine sleep score with stress.
- Verification: a regression test encodes this dependency distinction; full mobile and Wear unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass on the final sources. Both APKs remain unsigned (`apksigner` reports missing `META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`); ADB reports no connected devices. No install, clinical/provider read, or publish was performed.
- WP1 remains open for audit of every score consumer and record-origin/interval semantics; source consistency is not clinical validity.

## Sleep detail incomplete-stage display — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `MetricDetailActivity` always computed chart values as session duration minus known Awake intervals. When stage data were partial, this mixed a full session window with incomplete awake observations even though the UI labels that view as a session window.
- Fix: detail chart/day totals use elapsed session time unless known stage intervals continuously cover the full session; only complete coverage allows subtraction of Awake/out-of-bed intervals. The score remains withheld for partial or unknown-stage data.
- Verification: targeted `MetricDetailAggregationTest`, full mobile/Wear unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. Device/OEM sleep-stage behavior and rendered UI remain unverified; user-facing completeness labels remain required.

## Historical chart denominator audit — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: cumulative chart average is calculated from rendered non-empty buckets and the same card shows `有資料分段`; sleep note shows the count of recorded wake-date nights; steps note says the period mean uses recorded days. Missing calendar buckets are not synthesized as zero.
- Decision: these are observed-bucket/day means, not calendar-complete daily averages. Keep that wording and counts; do not infer data completeness from a record's presence. No code change was necessary.
- Still pending: verify the rendered labels and empty/provider/permission states on device, and complete remaining WP2 query-boundary/cancellation acceptance.

## Health Connect page bound — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: generic `readAllRecords` rejected repeated page tokens and capped total records, but did not cap page count. A provider returning endless empty pages with unique tokens could loop without ever reaching the record cap.
- Fix: extracted a cancellable page collector, added a 256-page cap alongside the existing 25,000-record cap, and reject the entire result (no valid-looking prefix) when either cap is exceeded. Oversized reads now map to a distinct user-facing “拒絕部分結果” state.
- Verification: tests cover unique empty-page exhaustion, record-cap rejection, cancellation mid-read, and the user-visible failure category. Full mobile/Wear unit suites, Release builds, and Release lint pass. Live Health Connect behavior and UI remain unverified.

## Wear stale-callback gate — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: service callback scheduling already required `activeSensorSuiteToken == callback token == sensorSuiteToken`; cancel/finish increment the suite token and clear the active token/tag. This rejects stale callbacks in source, but the invariant had no direct regression test.
- Fix: extracted the exact active/current-token condition into `WearSensorSuiteCallbackPolicy`, used by production scheduling, and covered current, stale, inactive, and invalidated-after-cancel tokens.
- Verification: Wear full unit tests, Release assembly/lint, and mobile full unit tests pass. Release Wear APK remains unsigned; current ADB scan has no devices. The Wear module has only JUnit (no Robolectric/instrumentation harness), so service lifecycle and physical listener/WakeLock behavior remain hardware-unverified.

## Research benchmark manifest integrity — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `verify_checksums` ignored every non-empty manifest line that did not match the SHA-256 format. A manifest containing valid entries plus malformed entries could therefore report `verified` despite incomplete integrity coverage.
- Fix: malformed non-empty entries and duplicate normalized paths now produce a failed checksum state with bounded diagnostics; regressions prove valid files cannot mask invalid rows or duplicate entries. Paired-session preflight now enforces schema enums/boolean types, strict RFC 3339 times, UUIDs, finite sampling metadata, consent-before-measurement, and documented mapping/calibration/time-basis conditions for comparison.
- Verification: all 38 research unit tests and `py_compile` pass; the updated JSON Schema parses successfully. A fresh offline benchmark verified 59/59 dataset checksums and processed 19/19 records / 218 reference-valid windows. Baselines remain unsuitable: participant macro-MAE 38.52 bpm (peak count) and 44.93 bpm (spectral); exploratory candidate rule accepted 0/218, with no manual SQI labels. This supports failure analysis only, not watch calibration or physiological output.
- Review clarification at that checkpoint: results were descriptive 30-second-window comparisons under unverified relative-zero alignment from only 8 participants, without offset correction or confidence intervals. The exploratory zero-acceptance rule combines detector and feasibility checks; it does not prove the dataset has no usable signal. Registry now labels 256 Hz as a nominal WFDB record rate and states that this is not shared-clock evidence. Participant-cluster intervals were added later under “Research benchmark participant uncertainty.”
- Next: continue auditing raw-data provenance and metric consumers; paired Xiaomi/reference acquisition and all device/release gates remain pending.

## WP4 source-window propagation — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: phone insights already carried timestamps for HRV/RHR, sleep, wrist temperature, and TRIMP sessions, but SpO₂ and aggregate-derived values exposed no input-period metadata. A new “fresh” threshold would be unsupported without metric-specific cadence evidence.
- Fix: insights now include actual SpO₂ observation bounds, the daily-energy calculation interval, the seven-complete-day activity-load interval, and the exact Health Connect query cutoff separately from calculation completion time. Phone display, Data Layer, Wear persistence, and Wear readiness UI carry these bounds. Missing fields are set to unknown rather than retaining an older calculation's timestamp, avoiding false provenance. No freshness threshold or physiological conversion was introduced.
- Review: independent review found the query cutoff/completion-time gap and legacy packet field omission. Both are addressed; missing legacy fields intentionally clear to 0/“時間未提供” because preserving old source times could misattribute prior inputs to the incoming result.
- Verification: targeted phone and Wear timestamp-policy tests, full mobile and Wear unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. No paired device was connected, so Data Layer receipt/display on hardware remains unverified. Freshness gates remain `UNCONFIGURED`.

## WP1 oxygen-window semantics — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the low-oxygen sample percentage consumes all Health Connect SpO₂ samples from the queried seven-day window, while the unavailable UI state said “waiting for nighttime SpO₂ records,” incorrectly implying a sleep-only metric.
- Fix: label the result as a seven-day sample proportion, explicitly not a nighttime-specific measure; the missing-data state now simply says it is waiting for SpO₂ records.
- Verification: regression assertion added to `HealthInsightMeasurementTimeTest`; targeted mobile test plus full Wear unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. No device/live Health Connect validation was available.
- Limitation: this is a descriptive sample ratio, not ODI, apnea screening, or a clinical SpO₂ assessment. Sleep-only analysis requires associating samples with a validated sleep interval.

## WP1 step-regularity coverage semantics — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the seven-day rolling query groups step records by local date and sends only dates with records to a coefficient-of-variation score. It does not prove that each date is complete; absent dates were therefore omitted, not zero-filled, while the UI called the output “activity regularity.” An independent bounded source audit confirmed the exact path and absence of a full-week coverage check.
- Fix: phone insight and AI health analysis labels now say the value is a descriptive consistency rule score over days with step records, not a complete-week measure. Missing dates remain unknown and are not synthesized as zero.
- Verification: regression coverage asserts the new display wording; targeted phone test, full Wear unit suite, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Limitation: no device/live Health Connect check was possible. The underlying sample-day statistic is not a validated clinical or behavioral regularity measure; future work may withhold it or show actual day coverage if the product needs a full-week metric.

## WP1 SpO₂ multi-day score semantics — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `oxygenStability` collapsed the minimum/maximum across a rolling seven-day sample set and the fraction below 90% into an arbitrary 0–100 score. Samples could be from different times/days, so this score could not establish nighttime or within-session stability.
- Fix: removed that composite score. The phone insight and AI-analysis page now show the valid sample count and observed range; the below-90% percentage is retained only as descriptive sample proportion and explicitly marked non-nighttime. Values are accepted only when finite and in the Health Connect percentage domain 0–100; fewer than three valid samples do not produce a proportion. Removed the misleading WHO-emergency linkage for a multi-day sample proportion.
- Independent review found that the initial summary implementation incorrectly used 70% as a lower validity bound, which could hide severe values. Corrected it to the official 0–100 percentage domain; the reviewer also flagged and we removed the aggregate-to-emergency wording.
- Evidence: Android's official Health Connect `Percentage` documentation says the unit is a percentage, not a fraction, and the `OxygenSaturationRecord` builder documents valid range 0–100: [Percentage unit](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/units/Percentage), [OxygenSaturationRecord.Builder](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/records/OxygenSaturationRecord.Builder).
- Verification: unit coverage checks 0/low values are retained, >100/negative/non-finite values are excluded, valid count/range, and minimum count for the proportion. Targeted tests, full mobile/Wear unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Limitation: no connected device/live Health Connect review; this is not an ODI, sleep-apnea screen, or clinical stability score. A sleep-only measure requires validated association with a sleep window and appropriate sensor provenance.

## WP1 sleep-date coverage semantics — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: `sleepRegularity` constructed a 48-bin day for every calendar date between the earliest and latest session, including dates without any SleepSessionRecord. It then compared adjacent days; missing dates could become all-false arrays and contribute apparent agreement.
- Fix: schedule-similarity comparisons now require a sleep-record interval to intersect both dates in each adjacent-date pair. Skip DST transition dates whose local day is not exactly 24 hours, since the score uses 48 half-hour bins. If no eligible pair exists, return unavailable; never interpret a missing date as a no-sleep day. The UI describes this as an exploratory interval-similarity rule, not a clinical sleep-regularity metric.
- Independent review confirmed the missing-day guard and empty-comparison handling, and identified the fixed-bin DST mismatch; the implementation now excludes 23/25-hour dates.
- Verification: regression tests cover consecutive recorded dates, a missing intervening date, and spring/autumn DST transitions. Targeted tests, full mobile/Wear unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. ADB has no attached devices; both resulting Release APKs remain unsigned.
- Limitation: session records do not prove sleep was continuously detected or that absence means wakefulness; the score remains exploratory. DST transition-date comparisons are intentionally unavailable. No live Health Connect/device acceptance was available.

## Research registry and benchmark reproducibility — 2026-09-28

- Independent internal review found the sleep cohort description could be mistaken for the included sample size and that MIMIC-IV v3.1 was missing its version DOI. Corrected the cohort to 39 recruited / 8 excluded / 31 included and added the version citation DOI `10.13026/kpb9-mt58` plus the original paper citation.
- Added per-dataset source-check date, citation URL, and explicit content-rights review status. This distinguishes database license review from content/redistribution clearance; MIMIC-IV remains restricted, unaccessed, and not cleared for product use or external-model transfer.
- Benchmark JSON now fingerprints the exact benchmark source and registry and records Python/NumPy/SciPy/WFDB versions alongside dataset checksums. The resulting run verified all 59 dataset files, processed 19/19 records and 218 reference-valid windows; baseline MAE remains poor (38.18 bpm peak-count, 41.94 bpm spectral), so these are failure-analysis baselines only.
- Verification: all 41 research-data tests pass; full offline benchmark rerun passed. No dataset was newly downloaded, no personal paired data was collected, and the paired-watch protocol remains a design draft pending ethics/privacy/regulatory review and consent.
- Scope boundary: public wrist PPG is a small Shimmer cohort and MIMIC-IV is credentialed clinical data; neither establishes Xiaomi Watch 2 Pro calibration or medical-device performance. Review source pages before any future use: [Wrist PPG](https://physionet.org/content/wrist/1.0.0/), [Sleep-Accel](https://physionet.org/content/sleep-accel/1.0.0/), [MIMIC-IV v3.1](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/3.1/), and [IMDRF SaMD clinical evaluation](https://www.imdrf.org/sites/default/files/docs/imdrf/final/technical/imdrf-tech-170921-samd-n41-clinical-evaluation_1.pdf).

## WP1 metric-level aggregate provenance — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: Home requested steps, distance, active calories, and sleep in one unfiltered `AggregateRequest`. `AggregationResult.dataOrigins` reports origins contributing to the response, while validation was comparing each aggregate only against origins from that metric's raw records; a coincidentally matching aggregate could therefore pass without proving its origin, and the shared request obscured per-metric source attribution.
- Fix: issue each Home metric's aggregate with a `dataOriginFilter` derived only from that metric's unambiguous raw records; validate both the response's reported origin and its aggregate value against the raw non-overlapping interval sum. The algorithmic calorie path applies the same source-filter and response-origin check. Versioned the rule as `HC_ACTIVITY_SLEEP_PLATFORM_PRIORITY_V2`. No source mixing, inferred value, or origin preference is introduced.
- Additional composite-score finding: the ANS “body battery” is a locally calculated heuristic, not an OEM/device-reported measurement. It may stand alone only when its underlying ANS inputs share a source; a sleep/stress-derived fallback requires sleep and stress provenance to match. Code and test terminology now state this distinction.
- Evidence: Android Health Connect API documents `AggregateRequest.dataOriginFilter` and `AggregationResult.dataOrigins` as the request filter and contributing origins: [Aggregate request/filter](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/HealthConnectClient), [Aggregation result origins](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/health/connect/client/aggregate/AggregationResult).
- Verification: focused source/composite policy tests pass; all 370 mobile unit tests pass; Wear unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. Mobile release APK was rebuilt, but remains unsigned. ADB reports no attached devices, so provider behavior, UI, and paired-device acceptance remain unverified.
- Remaining: audit all score consumers and run provider integration with independent data origins/overlaps on a live phone. Overnight sleep is currently selected by session end date but compared to a calendar-day aggregate; Health Connect docs describe the aggregate metric and half-open time filters but do not specify whether sleep duration is clipped at day boundaries. Keep mismatches hidden until provider behavior is verified with a controlled integration fixture/live source. This change does not establish clinical validity.

## WP1 HR-reserve composite source gate — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the HR-reserve-weighted activity-minute calculation combines sampled heart rate with a resting-heart-rate baseline, but unlike TRIMP it did not require both records to share a Health Connect data origin. Cross-source mixing could therefore produce a score from unrelated providers.
- Fix: withhold the derived minutes unless profile, both measurement classes, and matching source provenance are present; add an explicit unavailable reason for mismatched origins. No provider-priority assumption or value substitution is added.
- Verification: regression tests cover same-source acceptance and mismatched/missing-input rejection. Full mobile (371 tests) and Wear (133 tests) unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. APK provenance is recorded in `docs/build-provenance-2026-09-28.md`.
- Remaining: policy-level coverage does not substitute for Health Connect provider integration or device UI verification; continue auditing all derived consumers under WP1.

## WP1 score-consumer source audit — 2026-09-28

- Audited current derived consumers: ANS stress/body-energy requires same-source same-day RHR/HRV plus a single-source fully covered seven-day activity-load window; sleep duration requires complete known stage coverage, and sleep/stress composites require same source; gait efficiency requires identical, non-overlapping same-source time coverage for steps and distance; TRIMP requires common HR/exercise/RHR origin, at least two in-session HR samples, and same-local-day pre-session RHR; HR-reserve activity minutes now require matching HR/RHR source; training monotony requires complete single-source daily buckets. Single-metric descriptive summaries are already constrained to one known origin.
- Review outcome: no additional unguarded cross-source composite was found in this engine audit. The resulting rules remain heuristic, and threshold/clinical validation is outside this data-provenance gate.
- Remaining: live Health Connect behavior, real app UI states, and provider aggregation edge cases still require paired-device/integration acceptance; missing or mismatched values remain withheld.

## WP2 Health Center error classification — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: Home's guarded Health Connect reads previously reduced typed reader failures to an exception class string. A page/record safety cap or invalid/duplicate record IDs therefore appeared as a generic short label instead of explaining that partial/ambiguous data had been rejected.
- Fix: Home record and aggregate read failures now pass through `HealthConnectReadFailurePolicy`; known integrity/cap failures get actionable descriptions, while provider messages remain omitted and cancellation still propagates. Added a regression for safe-limit rejection.
- Verification: focused failure-policy test passes; full mobile (372 tests) and Wear (133 tests) suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. Current unsigned phone APK hash is recorded in build provenance.
- Remaining: no connected device/provider was available to verify the rendered Health Center states, permission settings round-trip, stale and invalid-timestamp presentation, or actual provider behavior.

## WP2 no-record vs capability wording — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: for empty Health Connect reads the Home summary described weight as “no watch weight sensor,” HRV as “no RR interval,” and respiration as “Health Services has no data type.” Those claims do not explain an empty Health Connect query and conflate device capability/provider availability with actual record absence. A follow-up text search caught one remaining weight fallback in the expanded summary after the first UI correction.
- Fix: bounded list reads track successful empty responses separately from denied permissions and read failures. Home empty-state text reports “no records in this query,” “not authorized,” or “read failed”; it no longer attributes missing records to absent watch sensors/RR support. A pure policy test asserts these states are distinct; valid zero-valued records remain data, not an empty response. The expanded summary fallback now uses the same state as the metric card.
- Verification: focused state-policy test passes; full mobile (373 tests) and Wear (133 tests), both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. APK fingerprint is updated in build provenance.
- Remaining: status-policy/unit coverage does not verify actual view rendering or distinguish every aggregate rejection reason at each card; device and Health Connect provider acceptance remain required.

## WP2 interval integrity and period boundaries — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: cumulative detail conversion silently returned an empty list for a zero/reversed source interval, and sleep detail used `mapNotNull` to drop invalid sessions. Remaining valid records could then render partial totals as complete. Point and sleep period membership uses an inclusive start/exclusive end; official Android documentation specifies Health Connect `TimeRangeFilter.between` as `[startTime, endTime)`.
- Fix: invalid interval records now throw a typed data-integrity error that suppresses the full detail result; half-open point-range membership is centralized and explicitly tested at both boundaries. No partial prefix, duration clipping, or synthetic value is introduced.
- Verification: targeted tests cover invalid/equal/reversed intervals and exact start/end membership. Full mobile (375) and Wear (133) unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Limitation: Android API docs establish filter boundary convention but do not fully specify interval-record matching across boundary instants for each provider. No FakeHealthConnectClient dependency or connected device is available; exact provider selection at midnight remains a live/integration acceptance item. Reference: [TimeRangeFilter API](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/TimeRangeFilter).

## WP2 sleep-stage integrity wording — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: Health Connect specifies sleep stages belong within their parent session and should not overlap. The existing coverage policy rejected overlaps but clipped out-of-session stages before assessing them, so a stage extending beyond the session could be accepted as complete. Its explanation also omitted overlap and out-of-session violations.
- Fix: coverage now validates each stage has positive duration, lies fully inside its parent session, and does not overlap another stage before evaluating completeness. UI explanation names missing/gapped/overlapping/out-of-session/unknown stages. Invalid stage data leaves only the session-window duration visible and withholds actual-sleep scoring.
- Verification: new test initially failed on an out-of-session interval, then passed after the correction. Full mobile (377) and Wear (133) unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Reference: [Health Connect SleepSessionRecord](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/records/SleepSessionRecord).

## WP1 gait source-coverage crash — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: average stride/gait efficiency calls `SourceIntervalAggregation.hasEquivalentCoverage` with every matching Health Connect step and distance interval. After eligibility checks, the helper used `single()` to read the data origin, which throws whenever either legitimate dataset contains more than one interval; the insight calculation's outer error handling could suppress the entire analysis instead of just withholding gait output.
- Fix: compare the first origin after the shared eligibility gate has established each list is non-empty and single-origin. Added a regression with multiple adjacent intervals in both metric lists and matching coverage.
- Verification: focused regression passes. Full mobile (378) and Wear (133) unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Limitation: unit policy tests do not exercise the Health Connect provider or rendered error handling; live multi-record provider acceptance remains open.

## WP1 gait ratio integrity — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the mean-distance-per-step helper clamped every positive ratio to 0.1–2.5 m/step. That transformed out-of-range input into an artificial boundary result rather than exposing the derived ratio or withholding it.
- Fix: remove the unsupported clamp; return only finite positive `distance / steps`, with invalid/non-positive input withheld. Add tests for ratios below and above the old clamp and invalid inputs.
- Verification: focused algorithm/source-coverage tests pass. Full mobile (379) and Wear (133) unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Limitation: this remains a descriptive ratio, not a gait assessment or clinically validated measure; comparable source and interval coverage gates remain required.

## Research benchmark sample-clock gaps — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the official PhysioNet dataset description/paper documents the Shimmer per-sample timestamp channel as wrapping every 0–60 seconds, with nominal 256 Hz recording. On the existing local 19-record dataset, per-record median observed delta was 0.003662 s and 99th percentile 0.004577 s, while maximum gaps ranged roughly 0.03–0.55 s. The benchmark previously linearly interpolated across every positive gap and still emitted estimates, which could disguise missing samples as a continuous waveform.
- Fix: benchmark v4 withholds any 30-second window intersecting a gap over two nominal sample periods, adds a per-window reason and report counts, and keeps those windows in reference/coverage denominators. The two-period cutoff is an engineering input-integrity bound above observed 99th-percentile jitter, not a physiological or clinical quality threshold.
- Verification: all 42 research tests pass; fresh run verified 59/59 checksums, 19/19 records and 218 windows; 115/218 windows were withheld for gaps. Peak-count emitted 103/218 (47.2%) with MAE 37.11 bpm; spectral emitted 103/218 (47.2%) with MAE 40.95 bpm. Exploratory quality gate remains 0/218; no clinical/SQI validity is claimed.
- Sources: [PhysioNet Wrist PPG dataset](https://physionet.org/content/wrist/1.0.0/); [dataset methods paper](https://www.mdpi.com/2306-5729/2/1/1).

## WP1 observed step-day attribution — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the activity-regularity input grouped full `StepsRecord` counts by `endTime` date. An interval crossing midnight was attributed in full to the ending day; one ending exactly at midnight was grouped to the next date despite representing a half-open interval ending before that date. This can distort sample-day regularity.
- Fix: validated whole step intervals are assigned by their last included instant (`endTime - 1 ns`). If any interval crosses a local date, overlaps another interval, has invalid source/time/value, or daily addition overflows, suppress the regularity score instead of splitting or assigning unverifiable counts. Insight and AI UI state the required minimum as safely attributable dates and explain the withholding reason.
- Verification: targeted regression tests cover exact-midnight assignment, cross-midnight withholding, overlapping intervals, and overflow. Full mobile (382) and Wear (133) unit tests, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass. Device/Health Connect integration remains unverified.
- Limitation: this retains a descriptive non-clinical sample-day rule; missing dates remain unknown and are not imputed as zero.

## Research benchmark dependency reproducibility — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the research README described the environment as pinned, but `requirements.txt` used open NumPy/SciPy ranges and recorded no transitive lock. A fresh install could silently change benchmark dependencies.
- Fix: pinned direct requirements, added a complete tested transitive `requirements-lock.txt` and Python `3.12.14` marker, and made the benchmark report fingerprint the lockfile. Registry tests now reject non-exact requirements or drift between direct and locked packages.
- Verification: research suite passes (43 tests); `pip check` reports no broken requirements. Offline benchmark re-run verifies 59/59 checksums, processes 19/19 records and 218 windows, and records lock SHA-256 `7be26425c0ef09c6ef0fe0c5fa66e77c9d869a551ab3c29a61a2a3fec1d5eadf`. Peak-count baseline remains 37.11 bpm MAE at 47.2% estimate-emission coverage; it is still unsuitable as a pulse-rate output and is not Xiaomi validation.
- Verification follow-up: created a fresh temporary Python 3.12.14 venv, installed only from the full lock, passed `pip check` and all 45 research tests, then reran the benchmark with matching checksum, lock hash, 19/19 records, 218 windows, and 37.11 bpm peak-count MAE.
- Limitation: this clean install was on Windows only; cross-platform reproduction remains unverified. The dataset and user data remain unbundled/unuploaded.

## Paired-session reference calibration interval — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: schema v1 and the preflight validator required only `calibration_valid_through`. A reference instrument calibrated after a recorded session could therefore pass the “covers full measurement” check if its expiry was later than the session.
- Fix: schema v2 requires both effective-from and valid-through timestamps; comparison eligibility requires the calibration interval to cover the complete session. Added a regression for a future effective date plus schema-contract coverage. Protocol now specifies this interval check.
- Verification: targeted paired-session tests pass (including the previously accepted post-session calibration counterexample), and the complete research suite passes (45 tests).
- Limitation: this checks supplied metadata consistency only; it does not authenticate calibration certificates or verify reference-device records, ethics approval, consent, or clinical suitability.

## Paired-session channel/unit compatibility — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the preflight validated channel and unit enums independently. A metadata record could mark `RAW_PPG` as `bpm` and comparison-eligible if the reference used bpm, despite raw samples not being a pulse-rate endpoint.
- Fix: added explicit allowed-unit mappings per declared channel and a hard comparison rejection for direct RAW_PPG. Added regression coverage for incompatible, direct-raw, and valid raw-only records.
- Verification: full research suite passes (47 tests); Python syntax checks and `pip check` pass.
- Limitation: metadata is self-reported and cannot prove channel mapping; raw PPG requires a separately specified and validated estimator before a derived-rate study can be considered.

## Paired-session JSON parser integrity — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: the CLI used Python's default JSON decoder, which silently accepts duplicate object keys and keeps the final value; consent, eligibility, or measurement metadata could therefore be ambiguous to other parsers/reviewers. Input size was also unbounded.
- Fix: preflight loading now rejects duplicate keys at every JSON nesting level and reads at most 128 KiB. Added nested/top-level duplicate and oversize regressions; the CLI returns failure for duplicate-key input.
- Verification: full research suite passes (49 tests), all research Python files compile, `pip check` passes, and a direct CLI duplicate-key fixture exits nonzero.
- Limitation: this guards parser ambiguity/resource size only; it does not authenticate study metadata or documents.

## Research benchmark participant uncertainty — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: benchmark reports had pooled and per-participant point estimates, but no uncertainty interval despite repeated windows being clustered within only eight participants. Treating windows as independent would overstate precision.
- Fix: report schema v2 adds deterministic 10,000-replicate percentile bootstrap intervals for macro participant MAE and bias, resampling participants as clusters; intervals are withheld with fewer than two participants. Estimator outputs and acceptance rules are unchanged.
- Verification: 50 research tests pass. Full offline reports from the existing and clean Python 3.12.14 environments are byte-data equivalent after JSON parsing; both verify 59 checksums and process 19 records / 218 windows. Peak-count macro participant MAE is 36.87 bpm (95% exploratory CI 23.47–53.65); spectral macro MAE is 43.38 bpm (23.99–64.60).
- Limitation: only eight dataset participants; these bootstrap intervals are coarse, dataset-specific uncertainty summaries, not population inference, clinical performance, or Xiaomi validation. Full CI does not cure unverified alignment or estimator failure.

The phone reports READ_HEART_RATE, HRV, SpO₂, sleep, steps, exercise, and body-temperature Health Connect permissions granted. Watch BODY_SENSORS and ACTIVITY_RECOGNITION are granted. Both are charging, so no sensor scan was started.

Treat as a historical failure with current preconditions now observed, but do not call the scan path fixed until a fresh UI-started off-charge run completes.

Verify actual provider read results separately.

## WP1 TRIMP duration and baseline provenance — 2026-09-28

- Evidence: independent source review found TRIMP used the latest seven-day RHR baseline for every exercise, even when the resting measurement was after or on a different local date than that workout. The exercise-duration path also truncated intervals to whole minutes before formula use.
- Fix: TRIMP now uses the latest same-local-date RHR record strictly before each workout start; sessions without such a baseline are omitted and explained. Actual RHR source-time bounds are included in the insight. Workout duration retains fractional minutes from seconds/nanoseconds. No unsupported max-age/freshness threshold was invented.
- UI/tests: output states TRIMP uses an arithmetic mean from at least two in-session HR samples, same-date pre-exercise RHR, and age-estimated maximum HR; it explicitly is not time-weighted or a clinical prescription. Tests cover fractional duration, prior/local-date RHR selection, and timestamp wording.
- Verification: targeted tests, full mobile/Wear unit suites, both Release assemblies, and both Release lint tasks pass.
- Limitation: “same local date and before exercise” does not guarantee RHR is close enough to represent physiology at workout time; sample arithmetic mean can be biased if HR sampling is irregular. No maximum time gap has been established, and the score remains an unvalidated estimate. Device acceptance remains open.

## Next goals and watch-to-phone review synthesis — 2026-09-28

- Four available internal Codex reviewers (PM, Wear acquisition, mobile/data integrity, and QA/privacy/research) reviewed the next goals and capture path in parallel. Their proposals were reconciled against source and official Android documentation. This is not a claim that external Cursor/Claude/Gemini sessions participated; no raw health data was sent externally.
- Consensus: prioritize a traceable acquisition-to-display loop before adding algorithms. Keep “event received” separate from “physiological interpretation permitted”; failures, partial runs, cancellation, and unknown outcomes stay in denominators.
- Short term: classify sensor/Health Services registration, permission, zero-event, timeout, quality/calibration, transport, persistence, stale/duplicate/out-of-order, and UI-hidden states; test the phone/Wear payload contract and each consumer of derived waveform values.
- Medium term (by 2026-10-04): complete three non-overlapping, UI-started paired scans with the watch worn/off-charge and aligned app versions. For each run, reconcile watch event and sample counts, Data Layer delivery, phone persistence, process-restart readback, and rendered state; include failures and cancellation in the report.
- Long term (after 2026-10-04): add deterministic replay/fault injection, per-metric evidence-backed freshness, encrypted raw-export lifecycle tests, authorized signing/provenance, and—only under suitable consent/ethics/privacy review—reference-instrument paired research. No clinical claim without target-device validation.
- Capture contract: preflight run/session ID, app/build/API, permissions and device conditions; acquire in a bounded user-visible flow; preserve sensor event-time, declared unit/encoding and source; separately count callback/event, finite sample, retained, sent, received, validated, persisted and rendered data; record reason-coded gaps; verify after process restart; stop listeners/resources on every terminal path. Do not replace sample time with receipt/publication time or infer a physiological unit/calibration.
- Official API guidance supports selecting by use case: `MeasureClient` for short, user-visible spot measurements; `ExerciseClient` for active workouts; `PassiveMonitoringClient` for low-frequency, batched background monitoring. Passive batch timing is not predictable, and rapid measure listeners increase power use. Health Connect remains a phone-side store/read path with user-granted permissions and provenance, not a mechanism to expose arbitrary watch HAL channels. Sources: [Health Services](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-services), [background passive monitoring](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-services/monitor-background), [power guidance](https://developer.android.com/training/wearables/apps/power), [Health Connect read data](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-connect/read-data).
- New audit lead, not yet classified as a confirmed user-visible bug: `WearDataProcessor.enrichFormulasFromWaveform()` stores derived pulse/PI/HRV keys when waveform length and reported rate pass; separate consumers apply differing mapping/quality gates. Trace every consumer and enforce one shared eligibility/provenance gate before derived values can appear in a conclusion or be used by downstream health scores. Keep raw/research derivations explicitly separate. Relevant code: `mobile/src/main/java/com/jaysato/xiaomihealthbridge/WearDataProcessor.kt` and `HealthConclusionEngine.kt`.
- Device preflight after review: phone and watch still run installed v145 / 3.13.35; the local changed Release APKs are unsigned and were not installed. User confirmed watch worn/unlocked readiness earlier, but the later ADB screenshot showed the phone lock screen and USB charging at 93%; watch remains off-charge at 95%. No sensor scan was started. Re-check the foreground/unlocked state before the UI scan; do not bypass lock or use shell to start a sensor service.

## Cross-role review synthesis — 2026-09-28

- Six internal Codex reviewers covered PM sequencing, Health Connect aggregation, Wear acquisition, formula eligibility, and privacy/release; Cursor's eight-model task also completed, but its workspace was empty, so that is one summary-only review rather than eight independent source reviews. No source, raw waveform, device identifier, or health export was sent to Cursor.
- **Confirmed source defect — Wear final snapshot delivery:** `WearControlService` observes only final-snapshot `putDataItem` failure and logs it; no persistent retry/outbox was found before scan service teardown. Add bounded, duplicate-safe durable retry and failure/success tests before relying on completed scans as delivered. This is a transport reliability issue, not evidence of sensor or clinical failure. Review references: `wear/src/main/java/com/jaysato/xiaomihealthbridge/WearControlService.kt:1284-1288`; watch process teardown near `:1487`.
- **Confirmed source defect — sleep summary aggregation:** the seven-day sleep page sums complete session durations across all Health Connect origins, so overlapping/duplicate or out-of-window records can inflate the total. Reconcile by source and clip to the selected half-open time window; do not call session-window duration “actual sleep” when awake stages are included. Reference: `mobile/src/main/java/com/jaysato/xiaomihealthbridge/SleepSummaryActivity.kt:110-117`; reusable union helper exists in `SleepIntervalMath.kt`.
- **Confirmed source defect — sleep detail boundary:** sleep sessions are filtered by session endpoint and then retain their full duration, so a session intersecting a selected window can be omitted or overcounted. Clip session and stage intervals to the selected window and add midnight/week-boundary and DST tests. Reference: `mobile/src/main/java/com/jaysato/xiaomihealthbridge/MetricDetailActivity.kt:185-197,247-310`.
- **High-priority audit finding — derived-value eligibility is inconsistent:** internal and Cursor summary reviews agree on risk, but Cursor did not inspect source. Local source review must trace phone waveform enrichment, ECG/PPG formula persistence, every UI consumer, and exports; require one metric/source eligibility policy. Do not set `channelMappingVerified=true` without evidence. Keep ECG, optical PPG, LifeQ and RR paths separated; unknown calibration remains research-only.
- **Privacy/release gate:** QA reviewer found app-layer encryption absent for stored raw results/exports and current release APKs fail `apksigner verify`; no signing key was exposed or changed. Treat encryption lifecycle and authorized signer provenance as release blockers, not as repaired.
- **Order of work:** (1) fix source-confirmed sleep counting/boundaries with regression tests; (2) design and test bounded Wear final-snapshot retry with idempotency and quota; (3) unify derived-value eligibility and test all consumers; (4) rerun paired UI scans and reconcile event→transport→persistence→restart→UI; (5) only then validate encrypted exports and authorized signing. No device scan, install, or release occurred during this review.

## Wear final-snapshot delivery design — 2026-09-29

- Three internal reviewers (PM, QA and privacy/security) independently agreed that `putDataItem` success is only Data Layer acceptance, not proof of phone commit; retry must reuse the exact DataItem path and payload. No source or health data was sent externally.
- **Delivery contract:** `queued_encrypted → Data Layer accepted → phone committed/rejected → ACK received`. The phone ACKs only after a durable terminal receipt (accepted/persisted or explicitly rejected) and DataItem removal; Wear must not delete its encrypted outbox item on Data Layer task success alone.
- **Implementation boundary:** final snapshots only (never previews); serialize the DataMap once, assign a stable snapshot ID/path, persist an AES-GCM encrypted envelope in app-private no-backup storage using an Android Keystore key before first send, and use unique WorkManager drain/retry with exponential backoff. Bind schema/path/ID as authenticated metadata; cap each item, count and total bytes; on quota/key/cipher failure preserve existing pending items and expose a reason—no silent eviction. ACK is correlated by snapshot ID and idempotent.
- **Acceptance:** codec round trip, tamper/truncation and schema rejection, stable-path retry, atomic file write, quota behavior, process restart, repeated/unknown ACK, phone commit failure (no ACK), duplicate DataItem replay, and success/rejection ACK. Instrumented paired-device tests remain required for Keystore persistence, WorkManager/Doze timing, Data Layer disconnect/reconnect and phone durable commit. Until those pass, status copy must not claim end-to-end delivery.

## Sleep interval aggregation fix — 2026-09-28

- **Changed:** the seven-day sleep summary now clips sessions to the query window and unions overlapping/duplicate windows across providers instead of summing full record durations. UI wording now calls this “sleep session time” and warns that it can include awake time; the recent-session list is ordered and shows clipped durations.
- **Changed:** sleep detail clips session, awake, and stage intervals to the selected half-open window. It attributes an end exactly at midnight to the previous included local date using `end - 1 ns`, and reports clipped boundary sessions rather than omitting or counting the whole session.
- **Regression coverage:** added selected-window session/stage clipping, no-intersection, and midnight wake-date tests. Existing union tests cover overlapping, duplicate, and out-of-window intervals.
- **Verification:** targeted `MetricDetailAggregationTest` and `SleepIntervalMathTest` passed; full `:mobile:testDebugUnitTest` passed. This is unit-test evidence only; Health Connect provider integration and device UI were not exercised. No sensor scan, install, or release was performed.
- **Remaining review:** the Health Connect reviewer was asked for a read-only counter-review of this patch; response is pending. Next implementation items remain the durable Wear final-snapshot retry and the shared derived-value eligibility gate.

General health heart-rate/HRV conclusions exclude ECG-derived research values even if an old preference flag says otherwise.

## Step detail screenshot: empty steps from interval-overlap gate — 2026-09-28

- **Root cause confirmed:** `MetricDetailActivity` used raw `StepsRecord` intervals and applied a generic overlap rejection intended to prevent unsafe interval sums. Health Connect step records can overlap; rejecting the entire view produced the screenshot's “區間資料無法安全合計” card and hid all step totals.
- **Fix:** step details now use Health Connect `COUNT_TOTAL` aggregate APIs (hourly fixed-duration buckets for day view; local calendar-day period buckets for week/month/year views), which are designed to aggregate cumulative steps and avoid raw-record double counting. The screen identifies the result as “Health Connect 去重彙總” instead of exposing an internal provider package name. Other interval metrics retain the integrity gate.
- **Evidence:** Android Health Connect documentation recommends `aggregate()` rather than summing raw `StepsRecord` intervals to avoid double-counting; `aggregateGroupByDuration` and `aggregateGroupByPeriod` provide time-bucketed results. See [read data](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-connect/read-data) and [HealthConnectClient API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/health/connect/client/HealthConnectClient).
- **Tests/build:** step aggregate daily-bucket policy and related detail tests pass; full mobile unit suite and Release assemble pass after this change. Release output remains unsigned. Actual Health Connect provider results and installed-device screenshot require phone ADB/UI verification, not available in this run.

## Algorithm-output review — 2026-09-28

- **Decision from six internal Codex roles (PM, Health Connect, Wear, algorithm/data, QA, UI/UX):** lack of device-level clinical validation limits diagnosis/clinical claims, not deterministic or research calculations. Preserve a three-way separation: raw measurement; estimate with formula, input/sample coverage, and research/non-diagnostic label; clinical diagnosis (disabled pending appropriate validation). No external AI or raw personal health data was involved.
- **Screenshot/code findings:** valid SpO₂ samples were present and should remain visible as a sample summary; HRV/RHR-based composite pressure cannot be computed when those inputs are absent; partial sleep sessions must show session time separately from sleep-stage-derived duration; seven-day load cannot treat unobserved time as zero. Analysis-path raw steps/calories were being hidden by stricter interval overlap/source gates than the dashboard; cross-metric estimates such as gait efficiency still require matched source/time coverage.
- **Fixed this turn:** incomplete/unclassifiable sleep-stage coverage now yields unknown (`null`) for sleep duration scoring rather than a measured-looking zero; session time remains independently available. Added a regression test. Analysis technical details are collapsed by default. Added HC aggregate-backed daily step/active-calorie observations with an observed-day count and local date window; valid source-backed zero remains `0`, missing days remain `—`, and the subtotal is explicitly partial if the interval aggregate fails. Permission denial, permission-query failure, read failure, no records, and noninteractive deferral remain distinct. These totals do not weaken complete-window load, ANS, TRIMP, or gait gates. Code review caught and fixed the case where one successful daily query was lost when the interval query failed; partial valid daily results are now retained.
- **Workout pipeline audit/fixes:** Wear now collects workout interval deltas for steps, distance, and calories through the existing Health Connect bridge. Six-role review found and fixed three issues: cursor/summary only advance after successful Data Layer delivery with bounded retry; final `ENDED` metrics are processed before callback cleanup; Health Services `CALORIES` is written as Health Connect total calories (basal + activity), not active calories. Retry exhaustion is surfaced, not claimed successful. Official [Health Services CALORIES](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/health/services/client/data/DataType?authuser=0) and [Health Connect TotalCaloriesBurnedRecord](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/health/connect/client/records/TotalCaloriesBurnedRecord) definitions support the mapping.
- **Verification after integration:** full mobile (424) and Wear (147) unit tests passed; Debug and Release assemblies and `lintRelease` succeeded for both. Release APK signature verification remains separate; no signing key or signed release was produced. Mobile and Wear Debug v146 / 3.13.36 were installed on connected devices without clearing app data; both package versions verified. Phone is on USB power; watch is off-charge. No scan was triggered. Phone UI could not be visually accepted in this run because it remained black/locked, so installation is confirmed but screen rendering is not.
- **Code review status:** QA found no P1/P2 in aggregate policy or Wear retry/ENDED/calorie mapping. Asynchronous callback, retry exhaustion, Health Connect permission, and round-trip persistence are not covered end-to-end; real device acceptance remains required.
- **Next acceptance gate:** unlock phone and confirm watch worn/awake/off-charge; inspect installed UIs; perform UI-started paired scan; reconcile intervals from watch event through Data Layer, phone Health Connect write/readback, including retry and final callback; verify newly requested Health Connect write permission and total-calorie semantics. Keep gait efficiency source/time matched and incomplete seven-day load unavailable rather than filling gaps with zero.

## Algorithm-output review — 2026-09-29

- Six internal Codex reviewers (algorithm/research, Health Connect, UI, Wear acquisition, QA, PM) reviewed the reported missing metrics and algorithm gates. No external AI was contacted and no raw health data was shared.
- Decision: literature-based algorithms are appropriate for estimates when their required inputs exist; “not clinically validated” is not a reason to disable every calculation. Keep each output labeled by method, window, sample count, source, and estimate/research status. Do not infer missing inputs or turn estimates into diagnosis. Preserve device-specific calibration gates for PPG-derived SpO₂, ECG voltage, and BioZ composition.
- Confirmed over-gating/presentation issues: composite stress requires same-origin paired RHR/HRV plus seven complete days of activity calories; incomplete sleep staging suppresses a stage-derived duration score even where a separate session duration can be shown; one overall confidence/gating decision can obscure independently valid metrics. The daily activity aggregate is useful for displayed totals but must not be substituted into metrics requiring matched raw intervals.
- Confirmed Health Connect defect fixed: an aggregate read failure could be reported as `AVAILABLE` when the other query returned evidence. Partial query failure now remains `PARTIAL`; if interval aggregate succeeded, its full-range total is retained while failed daily breakdown is identified; if only daily values succeeded, the displayed value remains a partial subtotal. If both queries fail without evidence, state is `READ_FAILED`; genuine no-records remains distinct.
- Confirmed summary visibility defect fixed: global cross-input confidence no longer short-circuits individual analysis cards. When fewer than two core domains exist, summary scoring remains withheld as before; when domains can be reviewed but global confidence is insufficient, each review is shown while the combined score remains withheld. Added a regression test.

- **Algorithm availability review / internal meeting (2026-09-29):** three independent Codex review roles agree that “not clinically validated” must not be used as a blanket reason to suppress research/fitness algorithms. Current `AlgorithmicInsightsEngine.calculate()` deliberately returns null for stress, energy, recovery and readiness; TRIMP additionally requires one global source across exercise/HR/resting HR, sex-specific Banister coefficient, paired HR samples, and a same-day pre-exercise resting-HR baseline. This can hide eligible individual sessions when unrelated records have multiple origins. Decision: retain formula-specific minimum input/units/time-quality gates, but redesign outputs to calculate eligible sessions independently and show included/eligible coverage plus excluded reasons; never represent a partial sum as a complete 7-day load. Keep descriptive sleep-session regularity independent from stage completeness; show observed nights/coverage. Any new composite score must be a transparent, explicitly experimental wellness rule with inputs, weights and coverage visible—not a diagnostic or OEM-calibrated result. UI should put value/status/reason beside each metric and collapse verbose provenance into details. No code changed in this review round; targeted implementation and tests are the next engineering step. Official Health Connect guidance confirms cumulative step types should use `aggregate()` to reduce duplicate counting; step results must continue through the aggregate path rather than raw interval summation. Sources: Android Developers, [read data](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-connect/read-data), [aggregate data](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/health-connect/aggregate-data); Banister TRIMP primary paper, [Optimizing athletic performance by influence curves](https://doi.org/10.1152/jappl.1991.71.3.1151). External web AIs were not used; no personal data was shared.

Banister TRIMP pairs HR samples to each workout window and prefers a same-source, same-local-day pre-workout resting-HR baseline; only if unavailable does it use a known-source, same-day baseline from another origin, recording that provenance. HR-reserve activity minutes use known-origin values and are labelled as estimates. HR samples outside 30–220 bpm and resting HR outside 30–120 bpm do not influence source selection/calculation. The AI analysis UI reports TRIMP included/candidate sessions; partial results are not presented as full-week coverage. - **Data Layer replay cross-check (2026-09-29):** reviewed the six-role concern against the current processor and regression tests. The replay flow commits its checkpoint before requesting DataItem deletion; failed checkpoint does not acknowledge; crash-after-checkpoint retries acknowledgement; pending receipts replay after receipt-ledger eviction. `updateHeartRateFeature()` re-reads the latest timestamp/session/sequence under `FEATURE_LOCK` before committing, so a newer snapshot arriving after an earlier replay eligibility check is not overwritten by an older replay. Existing policy tests cover stale/equal rejection and replay durability ordering. No reproducible source-level data-rewind defect established; remaining gap is Android/Data Layer instrumentation or paired-device fault injection across the full storage/restart path.
- **HAL raw-value UI clarification (2026-09-29):** Android's Sensors HAL is the vendor hardware/software boundary feeding Android's sensor framework, not an app-accessible register dump; the `SensorEvent.values` array's schema depends on sensor type. In this code ECG event values are partly unpacked (first 10 values per event into a research waveform), timestamps/callback timing and quality descriptors are analyzed, and complete source values are retained/transferred; proprietary remainder remains uninterpreted because channel mapping/units/clock are unverified. Replaced misleading “HAL 原始事件（未處理）” heading with “HAL 原始事件封包（保留原始值）” and explicitly describe current shape/timing checks vs unavailable mV/bpm/HRV/clinical conversion. Unit test suite 452/452 and mobile Debug build pass. Debug APK SHA-256 `50F01495E421F601BC066F0057A27E5DB17B63F09242DCA510E1935BE2C632B9`. No ADB device is attached, so not installed. References: AOSP [Sensors HAL 2.0](https://source.android.com/docs/core/interaction/sensors/sensors-hal2), [sensor stack](https://source.android.com/docs/core/interaction/sensors/sensor-stack), Android [SensorEvent](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/hardware/SensorEvent). A future packet decoder requires vendor-published schema or verified paired reference capture; AI inference alone is not sufficient evidence.
- **Correction: ECG HAL payload semantics (2026-09-29; supersedes the “complete source values retained/transferred” wording above):** three internal roles cross-checked producer, mobile storage, detail UI and JSON export. The displayed 2,170 points are finite values extracted into `source_waveform`, not a count of full SensorEvents. ECG currently takes at most the first 10 finite values per event into the candidate waveform; `event.values.drop(10)` is stringified separately as metadata, with timestamp/metadata arrays bounded to the latest 512 rows. The detail UI shows only the first 32 candidate values; JSON export records point counts/metadata alignment, not all metadata rows. Therefore the app cannot claim lossless full SensorEvent reconstruction or that all HAL values are used. Corrected the UI to “HAL 解包候選波形值（研究用途）”, label count as candidate values, and state the 10-value extraction assumption and preservation limits. - **Algorithm meeting follow-up (2026-09-29):** six internal Codex roles (PM, Health Connect, data integrity, Wear, QA, research/security) reviewed the user's concern and screenshots. Decision: the user's premise is correct—literature-based algorithms should be used; device-clinical validation is not a blanket reason to omit fitness/research estimates. Implement/retain independent formula outputs when their own input contracts pass; do not suppress unrelated metrics because a composite score cannot be computed. Keep combined stress/readiness/body-energy scores explicitly unimplemented until their formula, weights, baseline period and minimum coverage are specified and tested; do not invent a score from whichever inputs happen to exist. The screenshot's 106 bpm is an observed HR, not established resting HR; observed Health Connect SpO₂ (96%) remains a measured source value, not an inferred value; absent HRV/RHR/sleep or per-workout HR blocks only the metrics that require those inputs. Per metric, show measured vs estimated, source/time window, observed coverage and a concrete reason (permission/read error/no record/partial/stale/quality/source ambiguity/not applicable). Literature supports activity-specific limits on wrist HR accuracy and warns that actigraphy sleep staging is not PSG; missing-data effects can alter HRV statistics, so avoid treating missing observations as zero or silently backfilling. References: [wrist-worn PPG HR systematic review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32552580/), [actigraphy sleep-stage systematic review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38384163/), [smartwatch HRV missing-data study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39993288/), [wearable stress ML review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36893657/). No raw health/device data was sent to external AI; these web sources were reviewed for method limits, not used as device calibration evidence.

- **HAL inventory interpretation audit (2026-09-29):** clarified that Android Sensors HAL is the OEM driver/framework boundary, not a direct chip-control API. HAL/API enumeration establishes exposed endpoints only; endpoint counts may include composite/fused outputs, vendor channels and wake/non-wake variants, and must not be interpreted as physical chip counts. The project has distinct paths: ordinary `SensorManager` motion/environment sensors; standardized Wear Health Services data; and Xiaomi/Google/LifeQ vendor physiological endpoints. Existing source has listeners/controllers for selected public sensors and raw probe attempts for listed vendor endpoints, but this does not prove current registration, event delivery, or usable health values. Prior exports recorded ECG/PPG/SpO2/BioZ payloads with unresolved mapping/units/timing/calibration; conditional motion channels had no event absent a trigger. The screenshot's “unprocessed HAL events” means opaque event values are retained, not valid decoded ECG samples. Official AOSP sensor-stack/HAL documentation and Wear Health Services guidance were checked; no external AI or health export was used. Current shell has no `adb` executable, so no fresh paired-device scan/log verification was possible. Next acceptance remains to reconnect ADB and compare, per channel, list → registration → event → watch snapshot → Data Layer → phone persistence → UI; retain raw/research status unless semantics are verified.
- **ECG raw-event contract repair (2026-09-29):** source review found a remaining fixed `SensorEvent.values.take(10)` “waveform” extraction and `drop(10)` remainder split. Although downstream physiology was gated, this could mislabel arbitrary HAL fields and omit the full event from a single raw preview. ECG no longer unpacks any field as a sample; the watch retains each callback's complete numeric `SensorEvent.values` row, publishes a distinct full-unmapped encoding, and records whether rows are complete, bounded to the latest 512 events, or rejected as a whole at the 32,768-character cap. Phone storage, raw detail UI, SensorSuiteDump, and explicit engineering export now preserve this provenance; old app-split records remain separately labelled legacy and are never reinterpreted as the new layout. Candidate sample counts remain zero for ECG; RR/HR/HRV, quality, mV, and packed point-rate calculations remain blocked. - **HAL explanation / ECG parser-contract follow-up (2026-09-29):** Android HAL enumeration is not a physical-chip inventory or a guarantee of app-visible data; successful registration, callback delivery, and physiological interpretation are separate evidence levels. Source audit found a producer/consumer bound mismatch: watch may preserve one complete raw ECG event row up to 32,768 chars, while generic phone `parseRows` had a 768-char row cap. Parser now accepts the larger row only when callers explicitly choose the ECG raw-event limit and otherwise retains the old bound; regression test verifies default rejection, explicit acceptance, and over-limit rejection. - **HAL screenshot reconciliation (2026-09-29):** current source rechecked after the user screenshot. “HAL” here means Android's vendor-driver/framework boundary; the app receives `SensorEvent` payloads, not direct chip registers. - **HAL / 空白數值稽核整合 (2026-09-29):** 使用者截圖中的「HAL 原始事件」是 Android sensor framework 傳入的 `SensorEvent.values` 原始陣列，不是直接讀晶片暫存器，也不保證欄位、單位或採樣時基已知。Android 官方定義 HAL 為 driver 與 framework 的介面；標準 `SensorEvent` 的陣列長度／語意依 sensor type 而定。專案能力矩陣列出的私有 MAX86178 類通道不能由晶片名稱推定 Watch 2 Pro 的 lead、ADC→mV、gain、clock 或校準。既有 UI / conclusion pipeline 將收到原始封包與可解讀生理值分開；不應為填入數字而放寬 PPG/SpO₂/ECG/BioZ 閘門。後續對每項空白值先分別檢查權限/註冊、callback、payload+單位、品質/校準、快照、Data Layer、保存、量測時間/新鮮度、最後 UI；空白須回報實際 terminal reason，而不可套用舊值或不同來源補數。可安全使用的通道亦須按型別界定（如官方加速度 SI、光照 lux、氣壓 hPa、步數累計），再核對硬體輸出和重複來源。證據：Android [Sensors HAL](https://source.android.com/docs/core/interaction/sensors/sensors-hal2)、[`SensorEvent`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/hardware/SensorEvent)、[Watch 硬體能力矩陣](watch-hardware-capability-matrix.md)、[HAL 驗證計畫](wear-sensor-validation-plan.md#L446)。本輪只有截圖和現有程式/文件檢視，ADB 無已連線裝置，未做實機掃描；沒有新程式修正或 UI 驗收。下一步是有配對裝置時按通道保存失敗階段及原因，先修取數/保存/UI 等工程缺口，再獨立評估可否進行生理解讀。
- **HAL 能力／程式接線交叉盤點 (2026-09-29):** 清單項目分四類，避免把「未使用」混為一談：(1) SensorManager 可枚舉但不是執行證據；同一型別的 wake-up、non-wakeup、校正／未校正、融合虛擬感測器不等於不同晶片或可用健康功能；(2) 已有 Android 標準資料路徑：加速度、陀螺儀、磁力計、旋轉向量、壓力、光照、計步等，部分用於特徵／導航或原始詳情，仍需按有效時間、來源與重複來源驗收；(3) 有專用採集路徑但只保留研究原始事件：PPG、SpO₂、ECG、BioZ、溫度／LifeQ 等，其中 ECG `SensorEvent.values` 尚未解碼，BioZ respiration 也沒有獨立呼吸率算法；(4) 只有能力目錄／UI 宣告、尚不能由程式或本輪硬體證據證明完整功能的項目，包含多個手勢、跌倒候選及返航等衍生功能。`ChipFeatureCatalog` 是功能映射索引，不是 HAL 實測證明；程式是否支援要看 `RawVitalsProbe.Kind` 精確 `stringType`、listener 註冊及該次 terminal status。來源：`EngineeringDebugActivity.buildPhoneReport()` 枚舉的是手機 SensorManager；Wear 清單／探測位於 `WearControlService`、`RawVitalsProbe`；功能索引在 `ChipFeatureCatalog`；底層 RAW 詳見 [Watch 硬體能力矩陣](watch-hardware-capability-matrix.md)。SDK ADB 本輪只回報 `List of devices attached`（無裝置），故此分類是靜態程式盤點，非本次手錶即時盤點；下一輪實機需分開列出「枚舉／註冊成功／收到事件／手機收到／持久化／UI呈現／可解讀」各層數量與失敗原因。
- **HAL 截圖用語／原始值整合補充 (2026-09-29):** Android 官方定義 HAL 是廠商硬體實作與 framework 間的控制／事件介面；一般 App 透過 Sensor API 取得 `SensorEvent`，其 `values` 長度和欄位語意依 sensor type 而定。故畫面「HAL 原始事件」較精確應解釋為 Sensor API callback 值，不是晶片暫存器 dump 或必然完整 ECG samples。截圖中 2,170 點與重複值 `48438` 僅可確認被保存的值數及樣貌，不能推定為 ADC 碼、飽和碼、mV 或特定錯誤；須取得 Xiaomi 對應 `stringType` 的欄位 schema／單位／時基，或以可控輸入與廠商參考工具驗證後才可解碼。標準 Android 型別（加速度、光照、氣壓等）按 SDK 約定可做一般用途；廠商私有生理型別需單獨確認契約，勿因端點出現在清單便標示「已使用」。來源：[AOSP Sensors HAL 2.0](https://source.android.com/docs/core/interaction/sensors/sensors-hal2)、[AOSP Sensors](https://source.android.com/docs/core/interaction/sensors)、[SensorEvent API](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/hardware/SensorEvent)。目前仍無連線裝置，本項只整合官方文件、既有程式與截圖，不是新實機掃描；未改算法、單位或校準狀態。
- **Health Connect 讀取失敗分類回歸 (2026-09-29):** 對 `HealthConnectReadFailurePolicyTest` 定向執行 6 項，全部通過；覆蓋無資料／權限拒絕／讀取失敗區分、ID 與上限拒收、I/O／IPC／服務／查詢錯誤、權限撤銷及取消傳遞。這只驗證本地分類政策，不代表 Health Connect provider 或實機權限流程；未修改程式。
- **分析新鮮度規則邊界回歸 (2026-09-29):** 靜態核對 `HealthAnalysisFreshnessPolicy`：時間戳須為正值、不可在未來、來源須為 `android_sensor_event_elapsed_realtime`，10 分鐘為分析輸入窗；`SensorDisplayState` 的 30 分鐘是 UI 顯示窗。定向執行 `HealthAnalysisFreshnessPolicyTest` 3 項與 `HealthConclusionEngineTest` 31 項，34 項通過。測試確認邊界執行一致，不能證明 10/30 分鐘適合各指標；兩個期限是產品策略而非臨床標準，需按指標用途另定並由實機資料驗證。本輪未修改程式；無 ADB 裝置，未做實機資料／畫面驗收。
- **註冊失敗清理錯誤可觀測性 (2026-09-29):** 稽核 `RawVitalsProbe.failRegistration()` 發現 listener／trigger 清理錯誤被 `runCatching` 靜默吞掉；註冊失敗仍正確回報，但無法從該路徑 log 判斷清理是否也失敗。改用既有 `SensorRegistrationGuard.cleanup()`，維持逐項清理且將各 RuntimeException 記為 `registration_failure_cleanup_failed`，不改變測量狀態或數值。`SensorRegistrationGuardTest` 11/11 通過；Wear 全套 169/169 通過，`:wear:assembleRelease` 成功。沒有連線裝置，未驗證 OEM listener 實際清理，也未安裝；`TYPE_ORIENTATION` 既有 deprecated 編譯警告仍在。
- **One-shot trigger 註冊拋錯清理 (2026-09-29):** Code review 發現 `requestTriggerSensor()` 若於註冊過程拋 RuntimeException，賦值式 `triggerArmed = request...` 不會完成，先前錯誤清理可能因旗標仍 false 而略過取消 trigger。現在在呼叫前暫存正在註冊的 Sensor，註冊例外與一般停止共用清理並逐項記錄錯誤；成功回呼後清空候選。Wear 全套 169/169 通過，`:wear:assembleRelease` 與 `:wear:lintRelease` 成功。無連線設備，無法證明 OEM HAL 在例外後是否留下 trigger；未安裝，需實機負向測試確認。

- **環境感測器時間來源端到端修正 (2026-09-29):** 稽核發現 Wear 特徵快照中的氣壓高度／環境光／方向值，在 `captureFeature()` 以手機服務處理時間覆寫 sensor sample time；即時 feature listener 的舊轉換器也會把不合理／未來事件時間壓成「現在」。手機 Health Center 僅看年齡，未檢查這些欄位的 timestamp source，可能將延遲或無效時基資料顯示成即時值。修正為共用 `SensorEventEpochTimeResolver` 轉換事件時間並帶來源；無效事件不更新 feature；raw snapshot 路徑用其事件時間／來源；Data Layer 與手機持久化保留每欄來源；Health Center 對溫度、方向、氣壓高度、光照均要求可信 `android_sensor_event_elapsed_realtime` 且未過期，缺資料時明示原因。壓力結論僅在高度特徵與來源新鮮時附加該值。新增手機來源時間／過期邊界回歸，Wear 共用 resolver 既有缺失／未來／offset 漂移測試。全 Mobile 504/504、Wear 156/156；兩端 Release assemble 與 Release lint 成功。這是程式／單元測試驗證，ADB 清單仍空；未安裝、未實機掃描、未驗證圖表或 Data Layer 實際內容；新 APK 未簽章，裝置端驗收仍待辦。

- **首頁／健康結論心率時間窗文案對齊 (2026-09-29):** 稽核確認首頁可展示 24 小時內「最近、非即時」心率，而健康結論只接受程式設定的 10 分鐘輸入窗；無新候選時原文卻稱「尚未取得心率」，會與首頁數字矛盾。兩處現在共用明確的 24 小時首頁歷史顯示窗；結論頁遇到 10–24 小時內已存 Health Services／手錶紀錄時，仍不顯示該值、不納入分數，只說明存在較舊紀錄並超出此頁分析窗。加入測試證明仍為 `UNAVAILABLE` 且不顯示 BPM。驗證：Mobile 508/508、Wear 156/156，零失敗／錯誤／跳過；Mobile Release assembly、Release lint 通過。該 24 小時與 10 分鐘皆為現行 App 顯示／分析政策，非臨床常模；ADB 無連線裝置，未安裝或實機驗收；APK 未簽章。
